# Validation — Per-Good Trade Network Spec v6.4, round 9

**Document under test:** `per-good-trade-spec.md`, 2,190 lines, MD5 `0989f4dc54d31514123eed24f0aae5c5`, version stamp 6.4.

**Census validated:** `claims-delta-round9.md` — 1,077 rows (`Y001`–`Y1259`, 182 numbers in the span unused), every row graded here, one row per ID.

**Method.** Each claim was graded against primary sources — the EU4 1.37.5 install at
`C:\Program Files (x86)\Steam\steamapps\common\Europa Universalis IV`, its shipped saves and
crash dumps, the readable saves, the reference implementation and instruments in `scripts\`,
and the prior-version directories `..\v1-laplacian\` … `..\v5-owner-agnostic\`. Measured
figures were re-produced by running the instrument; file claims were settled by opening the
file and quoting the line; derivations were graded step by step. Fresh instrument runs for
this round are in `scripts\r9\` (`measure6.out`, `props6.out`, `epsilon6.out`, `europe.out`,
`round6.out`, `final.out`, `verify6.out`, `redtest6.out`, `coverage6.out`, `fingerprint6.out`,
`apparatus6.out`); probes written for this round are in `scripts\r9\` and its slice
working directories. Twenty-two slices were graded by subagents against
`scripts\r9\BRIEF.md`; **every PARTIAL and every REFUTED below was independently re-run or
re-read by the lead validator**, as were all 42 rows the round-9 delta touched (the 18
CHANGED, the 1 REWORDED and the 23 NEW).

**Verdicts.** CONFIRMED = reproduced or verified as scoped. PARTIAL = part holds, part
fails, or the claim outruns its evidence. REFUTED = the sources contradict it as scoped.
UNTESTABLE = unsettleable with these materials; the row says what would settle it.

---

## §1.1

| ID | claim | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|

## §1.4

| ID | claim | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Y300 | α(g)=clamp((price(g)/P₀)^k, α_min, α_max), P₀=2.0 | **CONFIRMED** | file read | `measure6.py:62`/`final.py:37`: `ALPHA = lambda g: max(0.2, min(3.0, (PRICES[g]/2.0)**1.0))` — exact match to clamp((price/2.0)^1, 0.2, 3.0) |
| Y301 | α>1 makes demand superlinear, concentrating luxuries on rich provinces | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | `w^α` for α>1 is convex increasing, so demand share grows faster than wealth share — standard property of the formula in `build_sc` (`solver.py:169-194`) |
| Y302 | α=1 makes demand proportional to economic size | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | At α=1, `c(n,g)=Σwealth(p)/Σworld wealth`, i.e. literally the wealth (economic-size) share |
| Y303 | α<1 makes demand sublinear, spreading bulk goods toward populous regions | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | `w^α` for α<1 is concave, compressing wealth differences, so demand share is relatively boosted for lower-wealth/more numerous nodes |
| Y304 | α moves with vanilla price events in both directions, no smoothing | **CONFIRMED** | file read | `events/PriceChanges.txt` and others contain `change_price` blocks with both positive (0.1-0.5) and negative (-0.1 to -0.75) `value`s; `ALPHA` lambda recomputes directly from current `PRICES[g]` each call with no memory/EMA term |

## §1.5

| ID | claim | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Y305 | Gold is excluded by configuration. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | scripts\solver.py:41 `EXCLUDED = {"gold", "unknown"}`; :42 `GOODS = sorted(g for g in PRICES if g not in EXCLUDED)`. |
| Y306 | Gold-mine income is its own engine income category (INCOMEGOLD, gold_income field, GOLD_MINE_SIZE), not booked as production income. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | defines.lua:1137 `GOLD_MINE_SIZE = 40`; localisation\core_l_english.yml:106 `INCOMEGOLD:0 "Gold"` (sibling of `INCOMETRADE`, distinct category); common\achievements.txt:6000 uses `gold_income = 10` as a scriptable trigger field. |
| Y307 | Owner-agnostic wealth(p) is built only from base_tax/base_production/price and reads no income field, so gold income is invisible to demand. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | spec §1.3 formula block: `wealth(p) = tax_value(p) + trade_value(p)` where `tax_value=TAX_COEFF·base_tax`, `trade_value=goods_produced·price(good)`; no income field appears anywhere in the formula. |
| Y308 | Gold is inert in vanilla trade value (base_price = 0, goldtype = yes). | **CONFIRMED** | file read | common\prices\00_prices.txt: `gold = { base_price = 0 \| goldtype = yes }`. |
| Y309 | Whether the pre-split production-income field carries gold is unknown and irrelevant here; that's why §2.7 item 12 was dropped. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | spec §2.7: "item 12 was dropped rather than run: under the model's owner-agnostic wealth (§1.3) the per-province production-income field is not read by anything, so what it contains no longer matters." |
| Y310 | A good with zero world production this month has no graph because s(n,g) is undefined. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | spec §1.2 `s(n,g)=goods_produced(n,g)/Σ_m goods_produced(m,g)`; scripts\solver.py build_sc: `live = world > 0; S[live] = gp[live]/world[live]` — S left 0/undefined where world=0, matching V=price·world=0 too. |
| Y311 | A latent good acquires graph, value weight and survival-table entry the first month any province produces it. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | Converse of Y310's build_sc logic: once world[gi]>0, live=True and V=price·world>0. |
| Y312 | Activation is not local: a province produces exactly one trade good at a time, so a latent good going live replaces the old one. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | EU4 install history\provinces\1-Uppland.txt: `trade_goods = grain` — a single scalar field per province, not a list. |
| Y313 | On conversion month, both goods' supply shares s(·,g) renormalise across every node producing either. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | Follows directly from §1.2's shared-denominator formula `s(n,g)=goods_produced(n,g)/Σ_m goods_produced(m,g)`: the province leaving one good's numerator/denominator and entering another's renormalises both. |
| Y314 | The converting province is repriced, changing wealth(p) and c(n,g) for every good, not just the two. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | §1.3 `c(n,g)=Σ wealth(p)^α(g)/Σ world wealth(q)^α(g)` uses the one shared wealth(p) field for every good g (only α(g) varies), so any wealth(p) change moves c(n,g) for all live goods. |
| Y315 | V_g moves for both goods, reweighting every display, link value and AI score. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | §1.6 formula annotation: `V_g = price(g)·Σ_m goods_produced(m,g)  # per-good value weights (display, link values, AI)`; both goods' Σ_m goods_produced changes on conversion. |
| Y316 | Φ_w moves on activation because §1.6 runs DRAIN on that same wealth field. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation + measurement | Φ_w=DRAIN(b_w) built from the same wealth(p); measured directly by the coal case (Y076): 16/159 edges flip on activation. |
| Y317 | An activation is a world-state change on the scale of development/conquest; every graph is entitled to move on it. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | Direct restatement of spec §1.5's own concluding sentence, supported by the verified Y313-Y316 chain. |
| Y076 | Repricing to coal the 45 owned latent-coal provinces flips 16/159 Φ_w edges and adds 214.60 ducats. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement | scripts\r9\measure6.out: "latent-coal owned and counted 45", "coal activation wealth delta 214.6", "coal activation edge flips 16"; reproduced in scripts\r9\S06w\probe_4237.py ("variant 1... wealth delta: 214.6 edge flips: 16"). |
| Y077 | Province 4237 (latent-coal + devastated) counterfactual: dropping its devastation adds 2.40 ducats and moves no additional edge. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement | scripts\r9\S06w\probe_4237.py: pid 4237 confirmed in ROWS, in `ON_STARTUP_DEVASTATION` (level 20), and in the latent-coal set; variant2(devastation dropped)-variant1(retained) wealth delta = 2.40 ducats exactly, edge-flip difference = 0 (16 vs 16). |
| Y318 | Coal's base price of 10.0 is the highest in the shipped price table. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | common\prices\00_prices.txt: `coal = { base_price = 10 }`; next-highest is `cloves = 8`, all others ≤4. |
| Y319 | v2.1 held Φ_w unaffected by latent goods ("reads wealth, not goods"); true under v2.0's V_g-weighted aggregate, false after the operator changed to DRAIN(wealth). | **CONFIRMED** | file read | v2-drain\per-good-trade-spec.md:132 "`Φ_w` reads wealth, not goods, and is unaffected"; v2-drain:851 `Φ_ord = Σ_g V_g·order_g` "(v2.0 entry, superseded in v2.1)" — the V_g-weighted operator that gives a latent good (V_g=0) no weight. |
| Y320 | Coal produces nowhere at the 1444 start. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | grep over EU4 install history\provinces for `trade_goods = coal` returns 0 matches; coal is `is_latent=yes` and its trigger requires 100% institution progress, unattainable pre-1450. |
| Y321 | Coal's default trigger fires on Enlightenment (Manufactories branches need special flags): dev_discounting_tribal=20 or innovativeness 20, province's own institution progress 100, owner has the institution. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | common\tradegoods\00_tradegoods.txt:2183-2229: outer `OR={development_discounting_tribal=20, owner={innovativeness=20}}`; default `if` branch (reached unless `has_province_flag GER_specific_coal` or owner `has_country_flag earlier_coal_available`) requires `provincial_institution_progress{which=enlightenment,value=100}` and `owner={has_institution=enlightenment}`; the other two branches require those flags. |
| Y322 | The 58 latent-coal provinces convert province-by-province over years, not in one tick, so the graph grows as they do. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement + file read | scripts\r9\measure6.out: "latent-coal provinces 58"; defines.lua:455 `KNOWLEDGE_SHARING_INSTITUTION_GROWTH_MONTHLY=1.0` confirms per-province institution progress accrues monthly (gradual), and each province's own progress gates its own conversion (Y321). |

## §1.6

| ID | claim | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Y323 | V_g = price(g)·world sum of goods_produced(m,g) are the per-good value weights for display/link/AI. | **CONFIRMED** | file read + code read | spec §1.6 formula block matches scripts\solver.py build_sc: `V = np.array([PRICES[g]*world[gi] ...])` where `world=gp.sum(axis=1)`. |
| Y324 | For the wealth good, supply is uniform: s_w(n)=1/N. | **CONFIRMED** | code read | scripts\measure6.py: `ph(a,w)=run_drain(np.full(N,1.0/N) - cv(a,w))`. |
| Y325 | For the wealth good, c_w(n) is the node's world-wealth share raised to α_Φ. | **CONFIRMED** | code read | scripts\measure6.py `cv()`: `t=(w/w.max())**a; n=Σt per node; return n/n.sum()` — algebraically equal to Σwealth(p)^α_Φ/Σ_world wealth(q)^α_Φ. |
| Y326 | b_w = s_w − c_w, with α_Φ = 2.0, a hyperparameter. | **CONFIRMED** | code read | scripts\measure6.py: `A_PHI = 2.0`; `ph()` computes exactly `s_w - c_w`. |
| Y327 | Φ_w = DRAIN(b_w), the §1.1 operator with wealth as the good. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | §1.1 defines DRAIN over any balance b_g=s_g-c_g; §1.6 applies it verbatim to b_w. |
| Y328 | Φ_w is the graph installed in the game. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | Verbatim spec sentence, §1.6. |
| Y329 | Under Φ_w every node supplies uniformly, rich nodes are net demanders, wealth pulls edges toward the wealthiest. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | Verbatim spec prose, §1.6, directly following the formula block just verified. |
| Y078 | Both sink count and location move with the wealth field; α_Φ sets concentration sharpness. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | Verbatim spec sentence, §1.6. |
| Y079 | Scaling European development alone moves the sink count up and down before settling back at two. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement | scripts\r9\europe.out: sequence 2,4,2,3,5,3,5,3,2,3,4,3,4,3,2 across the sweep, ending "top of range: x2.457-x2.600 settles at 2 sinks". |
| Y080 | v2.0-v4.0 said the count "emerges from concentration"; v5.0 said "the count is set by α_Φ"; both wrong the same way. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | v2-drain/v3/v4 spec files all contain "...concentration exactly as per-good sink counts do"; v5-owner-agnostic\per-good-trade-spec.md:342-344 "Their count is set by `α_Φ`; only their locations are emergent... v2.0 through v4.0 said the count 'emerges from concentration...'". |
| Y330 | What the world state moves is where sinks are and how the map drains, which is §3.1's first goal. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | spec §3.1 goal 1: "World responsiveness. Trade direction follows the world's current state, never authored arrows." |
| Y083 | Measured at α_Φ=2.0: two sinks genua/hangzhou, c_w ranks 2 and 1, node-wealth ranks 4 and 12. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement | scripts\r9\measure6.out: "Phi_w sinks ['genua','hangzhou']", genua rank (2,4), hangzhou rank (1,12). |
| Y084 | Both sinks are properties of the world; orientation doesn't depend on node numbering. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement | scripts\r9\S06w\relabel6_myrun.txt: 180/180 relabellings return sink set {genua,hangzhou}, 0/159 edges moved in any run. |
| Y335 | With unit arc costs Phase 2's b-flow is degenerate: many routings tie for minimum cost, pivot path moves with node order. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation + measurement | scripts\r9\S06w\relabel6_myrun.txt: unit-cost LP, 40/40 permutations return a different optimal support at objective identical to 5.6e-16 rel. dev. |
| Y085 | Measured on the LP directly: 40/40 permutations return a different optimal support at near-identical objective. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement | scripts\r9\S06w\relabel6_myrun.txt (fresh run, `python relabel6.py 60 4242 7 999` from scripts\): "unit cost (former) objective 0.789420000928 max rel deviation 5.626e-16 / permutations returning a DIFFERENT optimal support: 40 of 40". |
| Y087 | The old sink set was partly an artifact of node order, and v6.0 said so. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | scripts\v61a.py (the v6.0→v6.1 patch script) `old=` block: v6.0's own §1.6 text read "**One of those two is a property of the world and the other is a property of the node ordering**". |
| Y986 | Phase 2 now breaks ties inside the objective with a cost symmetric in the arc, read from node wealth. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | spec §1.1 Phase 2: "Arc costs are near-unit, symmetric in the arc, and read from node wealth: a first-order term TIE_EPS·(w[u]+w[v])/2 ... plus a second-order generic term". |
| Y086 | Over 180 relabellings (3 seeds of 60) the orientation never changed: 0/159 edges moved, sink set {genua,hangzhou} in 180/180. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement | scripts\r9\S06w\relabel6_myrun.txt (run from scripts\, per brief's note on relabel6.py's path resolution): "pooled over 180 relabellings / orientation changed: 0 of 180 / edges moving: mean 0.00, range 0-0 / baseline sink set returned: 180 (100.0%)". |
| Y987 | On the same LP under the tie-break cost, 0/40 permutations return a different support. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement | scripts\r9\S06w\relabel6_myrun.txt: "tie-break cost objective 0.789746281866 max rel deviation 7.029e-16 / permutations returning a DIFFERENT optimal support: 0 of 40". |
| Y988 | The reimplementation minimising the old (unit) objective disagrees with the shipped solver on 26/159 edges; relabel6.py aborts on exactly that. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement + code read | scripts\r9\S06w\probe_unpatched.py: unpatched v5 reimplementation vs `drain.py` on identity permutation — "undirected edges present in both: 159, of those, disagreeing in direction: 26"; relabel6.py's own `ok=(s0==SHIP_S) and (len(e0&SHIP_E)==len(SHIP_E))` check would fail and `sys.exit` on this disagreement. |
| Y989 | A symmetric cost is required: a directional term 1−ε(w[v]−w[u]) is a potential difference, its flow total is Σw[n]b[n] for every feasible routing, so it can't break a tie. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | Telescoping identity: Σ_(u,v) x(u,v)(w[v]-w[u]) = Σ_n w[n]·(inflow(n)-outflow(n)) = Σ_n w[n]·b[n] by flow conservation, independent of how flow is routed among ties — standard potential-difference argument, verified step by step. |
| Y091 | Nothing this section quotes about the installed graph is conditional on node order. | **CONFIRMED** | file read + measurement | Verbatim spec heading/sentence, backed by the 180/180, 0-edge-moved relabelling result (Y086). |

| Y990 | Per-good graphs differ: tie-break cost is good-independent wealth, but need not separate a per-good LP's optima. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | Verbatim spec sentence, §1.6, opening the per-good-graphs paragraph. |

The only `ship_power_propagation` strings are the name `MODIFIER_SHIP_POWER_PROPAGATION` and flavour descriptions (`custom_ship_power_propagation_desc`). No define comment, no tooltip and no shipped file relates the two, and the spec cites no observation for the product. Scoped to the searched install, the negative holds. |
| Y384 | Ship trade power propagates only where the country has a ship-propagation modifier, at a compounded rate the model reads as share x modifier | **PARTIAL** | lead recheck: file read (`common/defines.lua` L1205; `common/static_modifiers/00_static_modifiers.txt` L1511 and the other `ship_power_propagation` sites; `localisation/` sweep) + document read (L827) | The re-scoping is accurately carried: L827 now reads "at **a compounded rate the model reads as** the propagation share multiplied by that modifier" and adds "their composition is stated by no file, string or observation, so the product is the model's derived reading rather than an engine fact" — and both ingredients are indeed file-stated (`TRADE_PROPAGATE_DIVIDER = 5`, `ship_power_propagation` in eight shipped files). What is not settleable from these materials is the leading half, asserted flat as engine behaviour: **no shipped file or string states that ship propagation is gated on the country holding the modifier**, and the census gives the row provenance `stipulated`. A live probe (grant/withhold `ship_power_propagation` and read the upstream node's power itemisation) would settle it. |
| Y385 | Propagation is strictly one hop and never chains | **CONFIRMED** | measurement (val62p2_b_prop3.py prediction using province_power only, not max_pow/total power, as the propagation source) | Predicting receipt from province_power alone (excluding already-propagated power) matches observed max_pow almost exactly for all 200 pure receivers (ratio 5.0/5.001/5.002 only); if propagated power itself re-propagated, this province-power-only model would systematically under-predict further upstream, which it does not |
| Y386 | A node receives the summed contributions of all its downstream neighbours | **CONFIRMED** | file read / measurement (val62p2_b_prop3.py) | Script sums `province_power` over all `m2 in OUT.get(n,[])` (all downstream neighbours), and this sum reproduces observed max_pow at ratio ~5 for the qualifying set |

| ID | claim | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| Y434 | DRAIN's float exposure differs in kind from v1's dense linear algebra, badly exposed to it. | **CONFIRMED** | file read (../v1-laplacian/per-good-trade-spec.md L399) | v1's own spec: four "mathematically identical" Laplacian solves of one dead branch return 0.37000000000000000 / 0.36999999999999988 / -0.86999999999999988, one edge orienting both ways across runs — confirms v1's residual exposure. |
| Y435 | DRAIN compares input-derived quantities (DEF, b, arc costs), not solver residuals. | **CONFIRMED** | code read (drain.py) | phase1 keyfn = (-DEF[v], beta[v], pid[v]) (L211-219); phase2 tie-break uses TIE_COST (arc costs, flowop.py); no solver-residual comparison in the code. |
| Y1011 | MP question is no longer bit-exact arithmetic but disciplined build/instruction-stream identity. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | spec L950-951. |
| Y995 | §2.3's two changes (tie-break + pinned tolerance) move desync from design to verification problem. | **CONFIRMED** | file read + cross-ref | spec L955-958; corroborated by §2.3's TIE_EPS/TIE_EPS2/tolerance-pinning content (L1140+). |
| Y1012 | No randomness in the solve: identical fingerprint over repeated runs, separate processes and six PYTHONHASHSEED values (0, 1, 2, 42, 12345, random) | **CONFIRMED** | lead recheck: measurement (`for s in 0 1 2 42 12345 random; do PYTHONHASHSEED=$s python fingerprint6.py; done` in `scripts/r9/lead/`, six separate processes; output kept at `scripts/r9/fp_sweep.out`) | All six print the same digest: `8b13dddb9a345aa6d1990cac70d64e0b649b01f8cab1a9ec41cf9c87faa1012c` at PYTHONHASHSEED=0, 1, 2, 42, 12345 and random; a seventh run with the variable unset gives the same digest. Six values, enumerated exactly as the document lists them. |
| Y1113 | Fingerprint is one SHA-256 over Φ_w + 29 per-good graphs incl. sinks/sources/promotions/fallbacks/Phase-2 objective. | **CONFIRMED** | code read (fingerprint6.py) | rec() builds "%s\|%s\|%s\|%s\|%s\|%s\|%.17g" from directed edges, sinks, sources, promotions, fallbacks, r["cost"] (Phase-2 objective), hashed with hashlib.sha256 — matches exactly. |
| Y1013 | Uniqueness margin 3.8e-8 worst per good, 7.5e-6 aggregate — 8 to 10 orders above unit roundoff. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement (round6.out, §2.3/3.6 block) | "per-good floor 3.765e-08 (copper)" -> 3.8e-8; "aggregate margin at alpha_Phi = 2.0: 7.527e-06" -> 7.5e-6 (alpha_Phi=2.0 confirmed shipped, round6.py A_PHI=2.0, spec L1738); vs ~2.22e-16 roundoff, exponent gaps are 8 and 10. |
| Y1014 | Orientation under LP column permutation: 0 flips on the aggregate and all 29 goods, objective spread 1.1e-15 | **CONFIRMED** | lead recheck: measurement (ran the instrument that produces this figure, `scripts/p3_net.py`, whose docstring names Y1014; cross-checked against `p3_perm.py`) | `p3_net.py` prints "max objective relative spread, 21 column orders x 30 solves: **1.127e-15** (at livestock)" — 1.1e-15 to the quoted precision. Its construction (L61-67) is 30 problems (aggregate + 29 goods) x 21 column orders each, taking the worst per-problem relative spread. `p3_perm.py`'s smaller 6-permutation sweep gives 0 flips on aggregate and all 29 goods at `LP_OPTS` with a max relative spread of 1.03e-15 — a smaller sample of the same quantity, which is why a 6-permutation rerun appears to undershoot. The 0-flips half reproduces on both instruments. |
| Y1015 | Per-good \|net\| distribution bimodal: 2,321 edge-goods at 0, 2,290 above 1e-6, nothing between. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement (round6.out, §2.1 block) | "edge-goods at exactly 0: 2321; edge-goods above 1e-6: 2290; edge-goods strictly between: 0". |
| Y1114 | Smallest non-zero per-good \|net\| magnitude is 6.94e-06. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement (round6.out) | "smallest non-zero \|net\|: 6.939e-06" rounds to 6.94e-06. |
| Y1016 | ULP-level noise cannot change any decision this solver makes. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | Follows directly from Y1013/Y1015's measured margins (8-10 orders of magnitude above ~2.22e-16 double roundoff and above the smallest non-zero \|net\| of 6.94e-06). |

Non-ironman by choice: achievements are off with any mod" — same design language present verbatim in v2. |
| Y1019 | Check 2: no runtime CPU dispatch in the LP solver, single-threaded — the live risk. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | spec L977-980. |
| Y1020 | Check 3: §2.8's cross-implementation orientation check, DLL vs reference, exact match. | **CONFIRMED** | file read + cross-ref | spec L981-984; corroborated by §2.8's "Cross-implementation" row (L1475 area): "The DLL and the reference implementation agree on orientation exactly for every save in the historical set". |

Kept at `scripts/r9/quant_rerun.out`. |

| Y447 | Cost per good is one uncapacitated min-cost flow on 80 nodes and 318 arcs plus an O(V+E) sweep | **PARTIAL** | lead recheck: file read (`scripts/drain.py` L203-260 `sweep_priority`, L276-297 `run_drain`, L143-165 `sweep`) plus arithmetic check on the flow sizing | The flow sizing is right: 80 nodes and 2 x 159 = 318 arcs (`flowop.ARCS`). The sweep complexity is not, for the code that actually runs. `run_drain`'s default is `deterministic=True` (L276), which calls `sweep_priority` (L290) — a Kahn sweep with a `heapq` priority ready-queue (L209 `import heapq`, L232 `heapq.heappush`, L236 `heapq.heappop`), i.e. **O((V+E) log V)**; its stall branch additionally scans all of `core` (L240). The non-default `sweep` (L143) is a scan-order loop, O(V^2) worst case. Neither path is O(V+E). The practical point — the LP dominates the cost — is unaffected. |

My own fresh batch records 7, 1, 2 of 12. Five distinct counts across three batches on one machine; the interval is not reproducible. |

## §2.2a

| ID | claim | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Y454 | v2 called the target "map-agnostic" while proving its central properties only for the map it was measured on | **CONFIRMED** | file read (v2 spec vs v3/v6 characterization) | v2-drain/per-good-trade-spec.md:5 "...map-agnostic"; v2's DAG/acyclicity claims ("acyclic by theorem", L42; "Measured: acyclic", L58-59) carry no stated connectivity/min-degree premise, backed only by the single vanilla measurement. |
| Y455 | Premise 1: node graph connected; reachability=LP feasibility; feasible because Σ_n b_g(n)=0 identically | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | spec.md:1057-1058 verbatim; on a connected uncapacitated graph, any b summing to 0 is always LP-feasible (standard flow-conservation fact), matching flowop.py/toys.py's formulation. |
| Y456 | On >1 component, global balance is not enough — each component must balance separately; share normalisation doesn't deliver that | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | spec.md:1058-1059 verbatim; a single global normalization cannot guarantee per-component zero-sum; toys.py T5 constructs exactly this (2 components, globally balanced, per-component imbalanced). |
| Y457 | Vanilla 1444 is one component | **CONFIRMED** | measurement | props6.out: "components=1". |
| Y458 | Solver must compute components once at load; proceed on 1 component, renormalise-or-refuse on >1 | **CONFIRMED** | file read | spec.md:1063-1066 matches verbatim. |
| Y459 | The solver must not silently hand an infeasible program to the LP | **CONFIRMED** | file read | spec.md:1066 matches verbatim. |
| Y460 | v1 carried per-component renormalisation; v2 dropped it without replacement; v3 restores the requirement | **CONFIRMED** | file read (v1/v2/v3 comparison) | v1-laplacian/per-good-trade-spec.md:31 "Solve per connected component, renormalizing s and c..."; v2-drain/per-good-trade-spec.md: zero hits for renormal\|per-component; v3-owner-agnostic/per-good-trade-spec.md:89-90,494-503 restores it. |
| Y461 | Premise 2: Phase 0 no-op, or map-dependent properties read as measurements; several §1.1 props hold where Phase0 removes nothing (min deg≥2, no bridges, true of vanilla) | **CONFIRMED** | file read + measurement | spec.md:1070-1072 matches verbatim; props6.out "min degree 2 max 8 mean 3.98 ... bridges=0". |
| Y462 | Where Phase 0 acts, three properties weaken and the spec says so rather than asserting through it | **CONFIRMED** | arithmetic check | spec.md §2.2a table: of 4 rows, Global DAG stays "still proved" (unweakened); Sink-set equality, Marking order, and (half of) Free-edge determinism each weaken — 3 of 4. |
| Y463 | Global DAG proved on 2-core map, still proved where Phase 0 acts, because pendant edges are bridges and cannot close a cycle | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check + engine test | spec.md table row verbatim; a bridge cannot lie on any cycle (graph theory); reproduced via toys.py T1 (pendant leaf C on a triangle) — resulting directed edges are acyclic. |
| Y464 | Sink-set equality measured exact 29/29 on a 2-core map; fails where Phase 0 acts because a pendant net-importer is a sink outside the set | **CONFIRMED** | measurement + engine test | props6.out "sink-set identity holds on 29 of 29 goods; failures []"; toys.py T1 reproduces the failure mode: actual sinks={C}, formula set {S0∩ft}∪promoted={B} — not equal. |
| Y465 | Marking order reproduces the DAG on a 2-core map; fails where Phase 0 acts because pendants have no marking order | **CONFIRMED** | file read + code read | spec.md table row verbatim; in drain.py/toys.py, peeled pendant nodes are excluded from `core` and never receive an `order[]` entry from the Phase-3 sweep. |
| Y135 | Where Phase 0 acts, free-edge determinism unaffected but index-independence not: key reads post-fold β, peeling can create ties raw balances lack | **CONFIRMED** | file read | spec.md table row verbatim (elided); consistent with drain.py's Phase 0 folding beta[u]+=beta[v] before the (DEF,β,index) key is read in Phase 3. |
| Y466 | Two cases independent of Phase 0 both break sink-set equality inside the 2-core (selected demander loses sinkhood via free edge; fallback promotion is an unset sink) | **CONFIRMED** | measurement + engine test | toys.py T2 (5-cycle+chord, no pendant): actual sinks={u2}, formula={u1,u2}; T3 (triangle, all degree 2): actual sinks={A}, formula=∅ — A in neither {selected} nor {promoted}. |
| Y467 | Stated target = connected maps; on a connected map with min degree ≥2 every §1.1 property is proved or measured-and-labelled | **CONFIRMED** | file read | spec.md:1087 matches verbatim. |
| Y468 | On a connected map with pendants the algorithm still runs and produces an acyclic, fully-oriented, demand-serving graph | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check + engine test | spec.md:1088-1090 matches verbatim; toys.py T1 (pendant present) yields a fully-oriented, acyclic directed graph, with the pendant's demand exactly served by Phase 4's un-peel. |
| Y469 | On a disconnected map the solver must renormalise per component or refuse | **CONFIRMED** | file read + engine test | spec.md:1090 matches verbatim; toys.py T5: a 2-component graph, globally balanced but per-component imbalanced, returns LP infeasible — confirming the necessity. |

| Y1115 | `\|w[u] - w[v]\|` telescopes: summed along a path it collapses to a function of the endpoints | **PARTIAL** | lead recheck: derivation check (constructed counterexample) plus measurement (`scripts/r9/round6.out`) | The telescoping step is stated without its qualifier and is false as written. It holds only on paths monotone in `w`: for node weights w = 0, 10, 0 the summed term is 10 + 10 = **20** while the endpoint difference is **0**, so the sum is not a function of the endpoints. The conclusion the step supports is nevertheless measured and correct — `round6.py` gives "shipped: goods with an alternative optimum 1 of 29 (paper); structured: 11 of 29". The argument needs "along a monotone path", or a cancellation argument that does not assume telescoping. |

## §3.2

| ID | claim | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Y589 | Two orientation families fail before DRAIN: first by theorem, second by measured exact rule; v2 called both theorems | **CONFIRMED** | file read + derivation check | spec.md:1505-1507; v2-drain\per-good-trade-spec.md:514 "Two families of orientation fail before this one, and both failures are theorems, not taste." confirms v2's wording; monotonicity-breaks-paths is a logical necessity (theorem) while the Laplacian sink rule is stated as "verified"/measured on 1444, not proved in general |
| Y590 | Local comparison (wealth, s-c, any ranking) is monotone, so no path can dip and rise; Malacca-Cape-Europe needs that dip | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | spec.md:1509-1512; trivially true by construction of a rank-comparison orientation; validation.md:3931 (v1) confirms cape_of_good_hope wealth = 0.00 (lowest in game), so any Malacca(high)->Cape(0)->Europe(high) path must dip and rise |
| Y591 | Monotonicity killed the RANK strawman: 17.1% of world demand unreachable (unweighted per-good mean; 7.7% value-weighted), Genoa crowned an unreachable cloves sink | **PARTIAL** | lead recheck: measurement (own probes `scripts/r9/lead/L_rank.py` and `L_rank2.py` running the shipped `rankop.py` on the current field, computing both aggregations; plus a rerun of `scripts/verify.py`) | The qualitative half reproduces exactly: under RANK, `cloves`'s only source is `the_moluccas`, `genua` is a cloves sink (out-degree 0), and `genua` is not reachable from `the_moluccas`. The figures do not. Measured on the current field: unweighted per-good mean unreachable demand **16.6664%** against the document's 17.1%, and value-weighted **7.5944%** against 7.7%. Corroborated by the shipped v1-era harness: `verify.py`'s cached `ranked :: mean reach` expects 83.29 and a live rerun gives **83.33** (= 16.67% unreachable) — one of 16 cached checks in that script that now fail against the current wealth field. |

| Y593 | v1's Laplacian sink rule is exactly (c-s)/deg > mean(nbr phi)-min(nbr phi), verified on every (good,node) pair | **CONFIRMED** | file read | v1-laplacian\diagnosis.md:229-244 derives the identity and states "Tested on every (good, node) pair: the criterion classifies sinkhood with accuracy 1.000 (2320/2320), zero violations" (80 nodes x 29 goods = 2320) |
| Y594 | Supply sparse vs dense demand sets the RHS via supply geography: spices in 18/80 nodes, cloves in 1 | **CONFIRMED** | measurement (S16w probe on solver.build_sc) | reran build_sc(ALPHA,eps=0.0): spices nonzero producing nodes = 18, cloves nonzero producing nodes = 1, matching verify6.out/coverage6.out "single-producer goods: ['cloves']" |
| Y595 | Under v1's Laplacian sinks landed where the field was flat, not at demand: highest-demand node never a sink, a zero-demand node outranked Genoa/Beijing, deleting demand variation left the sink unmoved | **CONFIRMED** | file read | v1-laplacian\diagnosis.md: genua c=0.035076 "highest demand in the game" and not a sink (saxony is); cape_of_good_hope c=0 outranks 66/80 nodes incl. Genoa/Venice/Champagne/Beijing (lines 192-198); CF1 (lines 334-338,350-351) deletes demand variation and saxony remains the sink |

DRAIN does not need it" removes v1's eps mix-in |
| Y157 | The ratio metric misses sparsity: most nodes produce nothing of a good (spices 18/80, cloves 1/80), so (c-s)/deg is dominated by where supply exists | **CONFIRMED** | measurement + derivation check | same build_sc rerun as Y594 (18 and 1 producing nodes); logic: with s=0 at non-producing nodes, (c-s)/deg is set by demand there but only differs at the sparse producing nodes, so a max/min ratio over producers alone is blind to the sparsity by construction |
| Y158 | On the contrast metric itself, demand is the wider contrast, not supply | **CONFIRMED** | file read | scripts\r9\final.out PART E "V117 supply vs demand contrast": spices supply max/min+ = 36, demand max/min+ = 471; grain supply=67, demand=309 — demand contrast exceeds supply contrast on this metric |

| Y159 | Better wealth inputs move Genoa to a co-sink at roughly x1.7 without making demand the determinant of placement | **CONFIRMED** | measurement (reran val62p5_c.py in S16w) | bisection on current field: genua minimal wealth multiple to become a spices sink = 1.724; direct sink-set probe shows {saxony} at k=1.724, {genua,saxony} at k=1.725 — genua becomes a co-sink, not sole sink |
| Y160 | Moving the spice sink to a Chinese node needs 3.6-4.8x that node's wealth: beijing 3.63x, hangzhou 4.13x, xian 4.61x, canton 4.78x | **CONFIRMED** | measurement (reran val62p5_i.py in S16w) | live bisection: beijing 3.626, hangzhou 4.125, xian 4.606, canton 4.775 — matches spec's rounded figures exactly |
| Y161 | These are wealth multiples not demand multiples: since demand = wealth^alpha normalised, the same move is a larger demand factor | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | c(n,g)=w(n)^alpha/sum(w^alpha) with alpha(spices)=1.5>1 (from (price/P0)^k, price=3.0); scaling wealth by k multiplies the numerator by ~k^1.5 (denominator only mildly moved since one node's contribution is a small share), so the demand-share multiple exceeds the wealth multiple k for any k>1 |
| Y162 | The four named Chinese nodes aren't cheapest: girin needs 3.89x, yumen 4.49x, both inside the 3.6-4.8x range | **CONFIRMED** | measurement (reran val62p5_i.py in S16w) | live bisection: girin 3.888, yumen 4.493 (both inside [3.626,4.775]); chengdu 8.088 falls outside the range and is correctly not named |

So the stated target is: connected maps" |

## §3.3

| ID | claim | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| Y618 | Wealth captures return flows for free: a sugar island becomes a genuine consumer of cloth and iron | **CONFIRMED** | lead recheck: document read (L1618) plus file read (`common/tradegoods/00_tradegoods.txt`) | L1618 now reads "a genuine consumer of cloth and iron", and both are shipped trade goods (L275, L802) while `tools` is not. The mechanism half — the production term carried by the trade good rather than by development — is §1.3's `prod_income = GP_COEFF x goods_produced x price` construction, unchanged this round. |
| Y619 | Sugar/cocoa/coffee are 1.2-1.6x grain; cloves/coal are the largest price ratios. | **CONFIRMED** | arithmetic check | common/prices/00_prices.txt: sugar 3.0, coffee 3.0 → /2.5=1.2; cocoa 4.0 → /2.5=1.6; cloves 8.0, coal 10.0 are the two highest base prices in the file. |
| Y620 | v1 and v2 said "negligible development but large production income". | **CONFIRMED** | file read | v1-laplacian/per-good-trade-spec.md:371 and v2-drain/per-good-trade-spec.md:564, both verbatim "a sugar island has negligible development but large production income". |
| Y621 | No colonial-nation, timeline or owner dependency in the current model. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | spec §1.3 formula (spec:305-309) reads only base_tax, base_production, price(good) and 4 province-state modifiers; no date or owner term appears. |
| Y622 | Wealth is for what the place is, not who runs it (autonomy/ideas/gov/estates/tech excluded). | **CONFIRMED** | file read | spec:281 "No autonomy, no production efficiency, no national ideas, no estate or government modifiers, no technology, no unrest." |
| Y623 | What remains moves: dev, goods, prices, and place-state modifiers. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | spec:1620 matches; cross-checked against §1.3's modifier table (devastation/prosperity/under_siege/occupied). |
| Y624 | Besieged province genuinely produces less; volatility is economics not noise. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | spec §1.3 table: `under_siege` grants `trade_goods_size_modifier=-0.25` into `goods_produced` (spec:~365). |
| Y625 | Model removes ownership-day volatility; a conquest doesn't change what a province demands. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | consistent with §1.3 formula excluding all owner-scoped terms (verified under Y621/Y622). |
| Y626 | Instruction: plan around the world, not the graph. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | spec:1620 verbatim "Plan around the world, not around the graph: the map is legible, not unchanging." |
| Y627 | Trade income excluded for circularity: would close a demand-orientation-flow-demand loop. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | model pipeline is Demand(§1.3)->concentration(§1.6)->DRAIN orientation->Merchants/Collection(§1.7-1.8, where trade income is realized); feeding that back into demand is a genuine cycle. |
| Y628 | Loop closes the long way: trade income funds development, development raises tax/production. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | identical wording present unchanged since v1-laplacian/per-good-trade-spec.md:375; standard EU4 development mechanic. |
| Y165 | cape_of_good_hope's 20 members include sea zone 1460 from map/default.map's sea_starts. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement | scripts/nodes.json: cape_of_good_hope members=[1460,789,833,...] (20 entries); map/default.map sea_starts contains 1460. |
| Y629 | Node sizes: 19 land provinces (cape_of_good_hope) to 77 (girin), a 4x spread. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement | computed land-province counts (nodes.json members minus sea_starts) over all 80 nodes: min=19 (cape_of_good_hope, unique), max=77 (girin, unique); 77/19=4.05. |
| Y630 | Node-level exponentiation rewards node size; luxuries would drain to coarsely-sliced nodes. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | for α>1, (k·w)^α = k^(α-1)·(k·w^α) > k·w^α, so node-aggregate-then-power overweights larger k relative to the per-province sum. |
| Y1221 | Shipped α(g) clamps cloves (price 8.0, live at 1444) to ceiling α_max=3.0, exponent 2.0. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement | drain.py/measure6.py: ALPHA=clamp((price/2.0)^1.0,0.2,3.0); cloves (8.0/2.0)^1=4.0 clamps to 3.0; measure6.out lists cloves as the sole single-producer (live) good. |
| Y1222 | At exponent 2.0 the two node-size comparisons read ≈16.4x and ≈4.25x. | **CONFIRMED** | arithmetic check | (77/19)^2=16.42≈16.4x; (68/33)^2=4.246≈4.25x. |
| Y631 | k^(α-1) distortion: ≈2x/1.44x at α=1.5, 4.1x/2.1x at installed α_Φ=2.0, between the two. | **CONFIRMED** | arithmetic check | (77/19)^0.5=2.01, (68/33)^0.5=1.44; (77/19)^1=4.05, (68/33)^1=2.06; both pairs sit between the α=1.5 figures and the α=3.0 ceiling figures (16.4x/4.25x). |
| Y632 | v2 said 77-node beats 19-node "of equal total wealth" by 2x; at equal totals node-level form ties. | **CONFIRMED** | file read + derivation check | v2-drain/per-good-trade-spec.md:573-574 verbatim quote confirmed; under a pure aggregate-then-power form (ΣW)^α, equal ΣW gives equal demand regardless of province count, i.e. count-blind tie. |
| Y633 | Superlinear demand concentrates on rich provinces; at α=1 per-province and node-aggregate forms coincide. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | Σx_i^1=Σx_i trivially, so exponentiation order doesn't matter at α=1; text carried unchanged from v2-drain/per-good-trade-spec.md:567-568. |

## §3.6

| ID | claim | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Y647 | A margin on orientation is a correctness bug: splicing two acyclic orientations from different ticks need not be acyclic | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | logical step verified: holding stale edges vs new-gradient edges is a splice of two orientations; Y648's numerical test demonstrates the splice producing a cycle |
| Y648 | v1 test: tol=1e-3, phi={0,0.0006,0.0012} tie-break turned an acyclic prior into A→B→C→A | **CONFIRMED** | file read + arithmetic check | v1-laplacian/per-good-trade-spec.md L395 and validation.md L4739-4749 give exactly these values; 0.0006\<tol holds prior (A→B,B→C), 0.0012>tol flips to C→A, forming a cycle |
| Y649 | Node-file format represents cycles fine: a list of named directed links, no acyclicity constraint | **CONFIRMED** | file read + engine test | mod/pgt_cycle/common/tradenodes/00_tradenodes.txt diffs from vanilla by exactly one added outgoing block (valencia→sevilla); its error.log shows the file was parsed (logged a declaration-order warning) before the later crash, i.e. no schema rejection |
| Y650 | Design depends on engine behaviour on a cyclic file, now measured: the engine dies | **CONFIRMED** | file read + engine test | verbatim in spec L1717-1719; 3 crash dumps independently read (see Y651) |
| Y651 | 2-node cycle: STACK_OVERFLOW at one address, 1002 eu4.exe frames, 3 launches; vanilla + 159-backwards both loaded/played | **CONFIRMED** | engine test | crashes/eu4_20260820_{134250,134617,165621}: all `EXCEPTION_STACK_OVERFLOW` at `0x00007FF6DDE6A8B4`, 1002 eu4.exe frames each, meta.yml `Mods: mod/pgt_cycle.mod`; mod diff vs vanilla 00_tradenodes.txt = 1 added block closing sevilla\<->valencia; v2-drain/game-session.md L104-108 records vanilla and pgt_permute (159/159 order violations) both "loads, runs" |
| Y652 | The dumps show an unbounded same-module recursion on the tested cycle | **PARTIAL** | lead recheck: file read of all three crash dumps (`crashes/eu4_20260820_{134250,134617,165621}/exception.txt`) | What the dumps show: `Unhandled Exception C00000FD (EXCEPTION_STACK_OVERFLOW) at address 0x00007FF6DDE6A8B4`, identical in all three, above exactly **1002** stack frames, every one of them the same placeholder line `eu4.exe (function-name not available) (+ 0)` — 1 distinct frame line across all 1002. Same-module is shown. *Recursion* and *unbounded* are not: with no function names and no per-frame addresses — which §2.4 L1264 itself states, "the dump records no per-frame addresses" — the trace cannot distinguish recursion from any other deep call chain, and 1002 reads as a dump cap rather than a measured depth. §3.6's neighbouring sentence (Y653) states the properly scoped version; this one outruns it. |

## §3.11

| ID | claim | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Y704 | Vanilla steering is outgoing-only; trade cannot be steered upstream at any power, per the engine's hint. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | localisation/hints_l_english.yml:234 HINT_TRADESTEERING_TEXT: "...You can never steer trade upstream or past your Main Trade City." |
| Y705 | The display is not outgoing-only: the node window lists incoming links as clickable entries. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | interface/tradeinterface.gui:90,110,519,528 declare both `incoming_nodes_listbox` and `outgoing_nodes_listbox` (TradeNodeLink widget, line 18); spec §2.7 item 14's own run confirms the incoming entry is clickable (navigates only). |
| Y706 | Because only outgoing links can be steered, "assigned" and "steering" are the same condition in vanilla. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | Follows from Y704 (steering outgoing-only) plus vanilla's incoming entries never accepting an assignment (only Y705's navigate-only behaviour) — no state exists in which a merchant is "assigned" without also "steering". |
| Y707 | §1.7 makes incoming entries assignable and pulls "assigned" and "steering" apart. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | spec.md:772-786 (§1.7): "what changes is what an incoming entry does — it must accept a merchant assignment..." and "assignment alone does not qualify" for caravan power. |
| Y708 | Caravan grant fires on two triggers with nothing checking value moves — an absence-of-strings finding, not proof; §2.7 item 6 settles it. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement | Python scan of eu4.exe (38,462,504 bytes) finds exactly `merchant_present_inland` and `merchant_steering_to_inland`, no third/value-movement string nearby; spec.md:1869-1871 states this as scoped ("not a proof... §2.7 item 6... settles it") which matches, and item 6 (spec.md ~2.7) is still unrun. |
| Y1181 | Both caravan trigger strings are verbatim in the binary's string table, and no third exists near them. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement | Regex ASCII-string scan of eu4.exe: 2 exact hits for the two trigger strings; of 20 total "inland"-substring strings recovered, none is a third `merchant_*_inland` trigger. |
| Y1182 | §2.7 item 6 is the probe that settles whether the engine checks that value moves. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | spec.md §2.7 item 6: "Does the engine grant it for a merchant assigned to a link that is incoming in Φ_w? For one whose link carries no goods?" — the no-goods case is exactly the value-movement check. |
| Y709 | Caravan tooltip reads as granting the bonus in the inland node. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | localisation/tradenodes_l_english.yml:17 TRADEMAP_INLAND_DESC: "...steers towards an inland trade node, will give you extra trade power in that node..." |
| Y710 | §2.7 item 11 settles the recipient with one merchant and two node windows; exposure surface is one of two readings. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | §2.7 item 11: "Merchant in a coastal node steering toward an inland one; read trade power in both nodes"; spec.md:1874 gives the two candidate exposure surfaces verbatim. |
| Y711 | §1.7's added condition is the right guard under both readings. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | Under either surface (~26 inland nodes vs every adjacent node), the bonus only fires when the merchant is steering (§1.7), so requiring "steering" (not bare assignment) gates correctly regardless of which surface is real. |
| Y712 | Caravan power is total country development ÷ 3 plus policy/idea modifiers, clamped to [2, 50]. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | common/defines.lua:1220-1222: `CARAVAN_FACTOR = 3.0`, `CARAVAN_POWER_MAX = 50`, `CARAVAN_POWER_MIN = 2`; `caravan_power` modifiers (0.10-0.33) found across common/ideas, common/policies/00_dip.txt, common/government_reforms. |
| Y713 | Nineteen countries are at the caravan cap from raw 1444 dev alone; Burgundy/Korea/Timurids/Portugal are 2-10% short. | **CONFIRMED** | measurement | scripts/r9/S20w/y713.py over prov1444.json (3,923 provinces, base_tax+base_production+base_manpower per owner): exactly 19 tags have dev/3≥50 (MNG...QAR); BUR 2.0% short, KOR 3.3%, TIM 5.3%, POR 9.3% — all within "2-10%". |
| Y714 | Caravan power does not scale with node presence at all. | **CONFIRMED** | file read | defines.lua's CARAVAN_FACTOR/MIN/MAX formula reads only development plus idea/policy modifiers; no node-presence or table term appears anywhere in defines.lua or the idea/policy files carrying `caravan_power`. |
| Y715 | Requiring the merchant to steer restores the vanilla state of affairs; granting on bare assignment would be an unintended deviation. | **CONFIRMED** | derivation check | spec.md:1882-1883 verbatim; follows directly from Y704/Y706 (vanilla never had a bare-assigned-but-not-steering state). |

Measured: `cloves` price 8.0 gives alpha = 16.0 unclamped, and its sinks are `beijing`, `doab`, `genua`, `kongo`; the cloves demand order is `hangzhou` (0.98751), `beijing` (0.00524), `doab` (0.00173), so the Chinese sink `beijing` is demand **rank 2** of 80 — a high-demand node, not a branch-end accident. And the triple is gone: v6.1 read "under alpha = 16 the cloves demand order is `hangzhou`, `beijing`, `doab`, and the sink lands on a..." (frozen L1772), while the current L1976 reads only "the cloves sink lands on a high-demand node rather than a geographic accident" — 0 occurrences of "demand order is" anywhere in the 2,190-line document. (The census row's phrase "demand-order triple" is the census's own name for that withdrawn list, not a string the spec ever contained, which is why a literal grep for it finds nothing.) |

- **Y1115** (§2.3) — The telescoping step is stated without its qualifier and is false as written. It holds only on paths monotone in `w`: for node weights w = 0, 10, 0 the summed term is 10 + 10 = **20** while the endpoint difference is **0**, so the sum is not a function of the endpoints. The conclusion the step supports is nevertheless measured and correct — `round6.py` gives "shipped: goods with an alternative optimum 1 of 29 (paper); structured: 11 of 29". The argument needs "along a monotone path", or a cancellation argument that does not assume telescoping.
